TP third-party notices

Dependency versions correspond to package-lock.json and src-tauri/Cargo.lock.
Review and update these notices whenever dependencies or enabled features change.

Includes npm production dependencies and the union of Windows/macOS Cargo graphs,

including build tools. Inclusion does not mean every package is linked into a binary.

Third-party materials retain their own licenses; TP MIT does not replace them.

For MPL-2.0 packages, unmodified source is available from the versioned crates.io

source/download links in each entry.

FFmpeg is optional and external; it is not bundled with TP.

Each package references the full license/notice texts collected at the end.

Identical texts are included once; line endings and trailing spaces are normalized.

UI icons use lucide-react under ISC, including the MIT notice for Feather-derived
icons. Both notices are preserved below. TP's original application mark is covered
by the project's MIT license. System fonts are not bundled.

TEST REFERENCE DATA (not included in the application frontend)

tests/fixtures/ciede2000-reference.txt contains the 34 numerical reference pairs
provided by G. Sharma, W. Wu and E. N. Dalal for the CIEDE2000 color-difference formula.
Reference: The CIEDE2000 Color-Difference Formula: Implementation Notes,
Supplementary Test Data, and Mathematical Observations, Color Research and
Application 30(1), 21-30 (2005). DOI: https://doi.org/10.1002/col.20070
Author page: https://hajim.rochester.edu/ece/sites/gsharma/ciede2000/
Original data: https://hajim.rochester.edu/ece/sites/gsharma/ciede2000/dataNprograms/ciede2000testdata.txt
These reference materials are not relicensed under TP's MIT license. The author's
page describes personal/research use and citation requirements for the MATLAB and
Excel implementations; TP does not distribute those implementations.

FULL TEXT 1aa0a34224480ff916817aafd48263c880fe53a1d116261a7bb8c952ee0173ab

# License

The licensing of these crates is a bit complicated:
- The crates `objc2`, `block2`, `objc2-foundation` and `objc2-encode` are
  [currently][#23] licensed under [the MIT license][MIT].
- All other crates are trio-licensed under the [Zlib], [Apache-2.0] or [MIT]
  license, at your option.

Furthermore, the crates are (usually automatically) derived from Apple SDKs,
and that may have implications for licensing, see below for details.

[#23]: https://github.com/madsmtm/objc2/issues/23
[MIT]: https://opensource.org/license/MIT
[Zlib]: https://zlib.net/zlib_license.html
[Apache-2.0]: https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0


## Apple SDKs

These crates are derived from Apple SDKs shipped with Xcode. You can obtain a
copy of the Xcode license at:

https://www.apple.com/legal/sla/docs/xcode.pdf

Or by typing `xcodebuild -license` in your terminal.

From reading the license, it is unclear whether distributing derived works
such as these crates are allowed?

But in any case, to practically use these crates, you will have to link, and
that only works when you have the correct Xcode SDK available to provide the
required `.tbd` files, which is why we choose to still use the normal SPDX
identifiers in the crates (Xcode is required to use the crates, and when using
Xcode you have already agreed to the Xcode license).

    means any form of the work other than Source Code Form.

    a separate file or files, that is not Covered Software.

1.8. "License"
    means this document.

1.9. "Licensable"
    means having the right to grant, to the maximum extent possible,

    (a) any file in Source Code Form that results from an addition to,

    means any patent claim(s), including without limitation, method,
